---

### Checked and found OK (do not touch)

Portfolio — chain reads and units
- `fetchPortfolioData` word layout: `mapUserInfo` is 7 words and offsets 0 amount / 3 claimed / 4 lockClaimed / 5 locked / 6 pendingToClaimed match `build-top-stakers.mjs` and a live read of the #1 staker (0xbfe2…: amount 18.97 B, locked 18.21 B, startTime 2026-09-23 decodes sanely). `balanceOf` INCLUDES staked PTGC (live: balance 19.180 B ≥ amount 18.966 B; the staking contract itself holds only 3.33 B = the fee pool), so `totalHolding = balance` and `walletFree = balance − amount` are right, and the holder-ranks list (PulseScan balances) ranks the same quantity.
- `parseWord` / `toNum` / `Number(BigInt)`: relative error 1e-16 → 2 × 10⁻⁶ tokens on a 2e28-wei balance; nothing visible. All reflection sums stay BigInt until the end (`computeUfoReflectionsWei`), `feeW/5n` is exact integer maths.
- PTGC reflections `max(0, balance + totalSend − totalReceived)` with all three reads in one `Promise.all`; a claim is a Transfer from the staking contract and sits in `totalReceived`, so it does not leak into reflections (comment at PortfolioV2.jsx:250–253 is right).
- The six-word `okWord` guard makes any failed PTGC read a red dot + retry, and the failure is not cached as data (a11). `agg` skips failed wallets rather than adding 0.
- `usd()` null-through, `fmtDollar`/`pfAbbr`/`fmtPriceNode` "—" on null/NaN, every `÷` on the page guarded (`pfPct`, `ps/tp`, `r.t$>0`, `all>0`, `total>0`, `den`); no `Infinity`/`NaN` can reach the DOM from these paths. `pfAbbr` picks the unit after rounding (999.995 thresholds).
- Per-wallet breakdown vs top tiles: both are Σ(balance) × the same `dp` price — identical up to a cent of float rounding; group subtotals and the ALL WALLETS row sum the same rows. Share bars use `r.t$/all`.
- League box: `pfLeague` on `PF_SUPPLY` (starting supply) — the same basis as index.html's tier modal default (`useState('start')` at index.html:12193/12345) and the same `LEAGUE_TIERS`/`BURN_C` percentages; `PfTierInfoModal`'s Burned/Circulating are the same `balanceOf(0x…369)` + `totalSupply` read as index.html's `fetchBurn` (live: PTGC 46.33 B burned, 333.33 B supply; UFO 96.04 B / 999.99 B), and a failed read prints "—" and keeps the starting basis.
- UFO reward-eligible supply (`pfUfoEligible`): one batched call, `supply − burn − contract − OA wallet − 13 pools`; failure falls back to the 10-min memo / localStorage copy, never to a guess; `elig>0` shape check on the cached object. Live today: 728.56 B eligible.
- `ATH_PRICES` identical on all four pages (PTGC 0.00133, UFO 0.000113 — the old contract's high, deliberate per gotcha 0).
- Price: `pricePair` is the same base-side / main-pair / volume-floor rule as index.html's `dsPricePair` (gotcha 35), main pairs hard-wired for PTGC and UFO, so the portfolio's price is the dashboard's. `quoteOf` returns null/null on failure; the header prints "—".

Portfolio — history, transactions, reflections windows
- Value history: balances at past blocks via batched archive `eth_call`, a point with any wallet unread is a gap (`v:null`), price = hourly within 3 h else daily within 2 d else gap; 1Y on 4-day UTC boundaries, 7D/30D/90D on local midnights (C2 — deliberate). Cache prunes > 400 d and old 6-h points; quota eviction drops the oldest 10 %. Block ↔ time anchors at 7/30/90/180/365/420 d, linear between — fine for day-level points.
- Recent Transactions: dedupe key `txHash|logIndex`; legs netted per tx; UFO sends grossed up by `feeLeg/5`, PTGC by the staking leg (the measured 94/5/1 and 2.5/1/0.5/1 fee splits); a Move between two own wallets is kept once and read from either side (`r.dir` / `-r.dir` via `cp`) — the rank window's in/out dots and the day net handle both sides correctly; Unstake matched on `withdraw` and the bare selector `0x2e1a7d4d` (A12 done); Buy/Sell require a contract counterparty AND another token moving in the tx (`pftDetail.swap`, A13 done); coverage reported to the oldest fetched item when the 20-page cap hits (A4 done); a failed price-file load is not cached (A3 done); 429 gets one polite retry.
- "Reflections today" / Day by day: Δbalance between midnights minus the day's own net flows, only when `pftCovered` for every wallet, else "transactions out of reach"; staking/locking counted as running totals (claimed + pending) so a claim day is not a spike; the per-wallet 30-day cache keeps the last ~100 days.
- Old-contract UFO reflections (`ufo-v1-earned` shards) are added for every counted wallet (sold-out ones too) and the split of today's bag is capped at 1 (`min(1, …)`), so principal can never go negative.
- Rank data plumbing: `rank-index` shard decode (`[firstDay,[delta…]]` → series) reproduces the top-30 ranks exactly; `pfrDate` is UTC; `r.t` in ms (the ×1000 bug is fixed); `logS` switches at an 8× span with the period's best as the top gridline; the calculator rejects ranks beyond the list and ranks at/below yours; "YOU'D BE A" uses the same `pfLeague`/starting supply as the League box.
- Share code: `GRAY-` + base64 JSON round-trips addresses (lower-cased), names and groups for ASCII/Latin-1; `sanitizeWallets` drops bad entries and duplicates on both save and load (a10).
- Races: `genRef` per wallet, `dropReflScans` on remove/refresh, the reflection queue result lands only on the same `gen`; toggling wallets re-keys every hook (`hAddrs` sorted join) and the 400 ms debounce absorbs Select-all storms.

Calculators
- Input parsing: every amount field strips to digits before `parseFloat` (so "—"/empty → 0, negatives impossible); `circulatingSupply` is null (not 1, not total supply) when the burn read fails and every MCap/share prints "—" (a6/c8 done; `LiveBadge` three-state).
- X-Multiplier: `basePrice = ratio × targetPaired`, `finalPrice = basePrice × X`, MCaps on `circulatingSupply` (null-guarded), goal seeker is a pure inversion; ratio = displayed price ÷ paired price (quote-side fix in place).
- Fresh Capital: fee taken on entry (`×(1−feeRate)`), staking share adds the new stake to the pool (`stake/(pool+stake)`), holder share does not change the supply — both correct for a new buyer; the Rewards tab's `rwdStaked/totalStakedPool` is correct for an existing staker.
- Moon Math: single-step x·y=k with 0.3 % fee, guard against draining a pool (`coreOut >= 0.999 reserve` → skip), pool weights capped at 1.
- "Market Cap" tile = DexScreener `marketCap||fdv`, the same source index.html's MCap uses; DexScreener tracks the 0x…369 burn (implied supply 287.0 B PTGC vs the page's 286.2 B, 0.3 %; UFO 904.0 B vs 904.0 B exact), so the tile and the X-Multiplier's "current MCap" agree to within 0.3 %.

Ledger
- UTC everywhere (month buckets, picker, default month, row times, `treasury-recent.json` gate `since ≤ oldest picker month`); `treasury-recent.json` covers 2026-03-01 → now (507 txns, 504 transfers, 0 duplicate hashes, 0 duplicate transfers across the two wallets); the four-file fallback and the v1 API fallback are intact and unpaged-complete.
- Classification reproduced on real data: PTGC buys match `dao-buys.json` hash-for-hash in Jun/Aug/Sep 2026 (see M-13 for the one July difference); burns are the delivered amount, not the calldata (a2: fee-on-transfer), summed PTGC legs for multi-pool buys (a4); PTGC arriving in a tx the DAO did not send is a Transfer with 📥, never a buy (a60); failed txs (`isError '1'`) excluded; wallet 2 limited to buys/burns/LP.
- Summary tiles sum the same `processedTxns` rows the list shows; "—" while loading/error/`tooOld` (7 d), amber past 2.5 h.

Charts
- Daily file 367 points, no gaps, UTC-midnight stamps; intraday hourly/15-min from the same GeckoTerminal candle convention as the live top-up (`c[0]*1000`, close `c[4]`), so `mergeSeries` (floor(ts/step), live wins) cannot double a candle at the seam; the last live candle is the in-progress one, as expected. Daily-tier labels and tooltips use `timeZone:'UTC'`; intraday uses local time, labelled "data to {time}". Stale file (> 36 h) is ignored rather than drawn. Token seeding `?from=`/`?token=` whitelisted.

Worker (where the page depends on it)
- Threshold is the month-entry sum decided at STEP 4 (`entry.usdAmount >= threshold`), never on a buy (a54); dedupe by `transaction_hash` across current+previous periods; `DATA_START_DATE` filter; `commissionPtgc = 0` for a below-threshold entry and the page respects the 0 (`getEntryComm`); the page's lifetime totals are summed from the monthly logs, not the unreliable `referrers[x].total*` block.

### Ideas (grounded in what exists)

1. **One `elig` for both pages.** `pfUfoEligible` already produces `{supply, elig, burn, wallets, pools, at}`; writing it to `data/ufo-eligible.json` from the hourly pipeline (two dozen `balanceOf` calls) lets calculators.html, the UFO dashboard's "share" copy and the portfolio read one number — and closes M-5 without a second exclusion list.
2. **Ghost-proof ranks for free with the data already written.** `build-holder-ranks.mjs` already keeps `top` with addresses; extending `top` to, say, the first 2,000 rows (≈ 100 KB) covers every wallet the League box calls Dolphin or better, so the self-dedupe in M-2 is a `Set` lookup for the wallets that care most, and the archive read is only needed below that.
3. **A "snapshot vs live" line in the rank footer.** The page already has `readAt` per token in the file and `pfAgo`; printing "list read 12 min ago · your balance live" per token (M-10) also makes the M-2 behaviour explainable until it is fixed.
4. **Execution price for Sell/Buy rows from data already fetched.** `pftDetail` fetches the tx's full token-transfer list for every Sell/Buy on screen; the WPLS/DAI counter-leg is in that answer, so "price each trade got" (M-8) is one division plus the WPLS price from `pfhPrices` — no new requests.
5. **Reconcile the ledger with the DAO Buys chart in the UI.** Both are built from the same two treasury files and agree hash-for-hash in most months (verified); a "PLS-paid buys N · token-paid buys M" split on the Ledger tile would make the July-type difference self-explaining and would let the chart adopt token-paid buys later.
6. **Rank history and holder list from one population** (M-3) is also the precondition for the TOP 100 window built 2026-10-22: its "where do you rank" today is `pfRank`, its history is the index — the same two lists.

## Raw sweep D

### Sweep D — Data pipeline, workflows, observability, resilience, scaling (Audit IV, 2026-10-22)

Baseline from the live `data/pipeline-status.json` (run 39854729320, `workflow_dispatch`, 461 s, 13 ok · 3 skipped · 0 failed):
lane coingecko-key 135 + 70 + 206 = 411 s · lane rpc 12 + 23 + 3 + 3 + 13 + 0 + 4 + 4 + **398** = 460 s · lane pair-volume 88 s ·
lane holder-ranks 274 (+ ~60 s rank-history once a day, + 32 s top-stakers every 2 h). `data/` = 296 MB (burn archive 219 MB in
seven half-year files, largest 47.3 MB = 2024-h2; rank-index 6,969 files / 33 MB; rank-history 20 MB; swap cache 7.8 MB).

#### D-1 · A history file that fails to parse is treated as "first run" — three scripts would then commit a one-row file or wrong totals
- status: POTENTIAL RISK
- priority: P2
- area: scripts/lv-snapshot.js:135–138; scripts/fetch-burn-history.js:901–912 (holder-history), 220–244 (period files); scripts/fetch-ufo-ptgc-burns.js:298–314
- problem: `catch (e) { console.warn('Could not parse existing snapshots, starting fresh'); snapshots = []; }` then `snapshots.push(snapshot); fs.writeFileSync(snapshotsPath, …)` (lv-snapshot.js:138, 140–141). The same shape at fetch-burn-history.js:906–907 (`console.log('Creating new holder history file...')`) and 313 of fetch-ufo-ptgc-burns.js (`'Cache load failed, starting fresh'`). For the PTGC period files the catch at 241–244 only logs `ERROR loading` and the loop goes on; `ptgcTotal = ptgcBurns.reduce(...)` (631) then sums whatever loaded.
- evidence: none of the 25 `writeFileSync` sites outside fetch-coingecko-data / rank-history / pipeline-run is tmp+rename (grep: 23 writes, 3 renames across the 19 scripts), and the Commit step runs `if: always()` after a `timeout-minutes: 30` cancel, so a half-written file CAN be staged. Consequences traced: (a) `lv-snapshots.json` = 921 rows since 2026-02-28 (402 KB, append-only, the L/V Analyser + Growth Projection percentiles) → replaced by a 1-row file and pushed; (b) `holder-history.json` = 275 daily rows since 2026-01-21 (the Holders 7D/30D/90D changes and sparklines) → same; (c) a corrupt OLD period file (e.g. 2023-h2 = 24.58 B of the 47.09 B lifetime, 52 %) is skipped forever — the file is never rewritten because its period has `burns.length === 0` — and `burn-summary.PTGC.totalBurned` drops to 22.5 B with `lastUpdated` fresh (the page's lifetime headline is the live chain read, so only the mcap fallback at index.html:3759–3762 and anything else reading the file is wrong); (d) a corrupt `ufo-ptgc-burns-cache.json` → "full fetch from each contract's launch": v1 from 2024-05-18 = 887 d × 8,640 blocks / LOG_CHUNK 2,000 = ~3,830 sequential `eth_getLogs` (+50 ms delay each, + receipts for 4,903 v1 burns) ≈ 20–30 min, against the 30-min job timeout, and the five rpc-lane steps behind it never run in that job; the 6 h gate repeats it four times a day until someone restores the cache from git.
- why it matters: the three append-only files are the only copies of data that cannot be rebuilt from the chain (DexScreener liquidity snapshots, PulseScan daily holder counts); recovery is a manual `git checkout <sha> -- data/…` the owner has to notice first. The probability is low (the writes take milliseconds), but the rule "an existing file that cannot be read is fatal" is two lines per script and removes a whole class of silent damage.
- fix: in each of the four places, distinguish "no file" from "file exists but unreadable": `if (fs.existsSync(p)) { try { … } catch (e) { console.error(…); process.exit(1); } }` — the step goes red, the previous commit's file stays, GitHub's failure mail fires. For the period files, throw inside the loop at 241. Optionally the shared tmp+rename helper c22 asked for (still open for 16 of 19 scripts).
- effort: Small
- risk: Low
- recommendation: FIX NOW

#### D-2 · Five builders carry a token/figure forward WITHOUT `::warning::`, so `pipeline-status.carried` and the ⚠️ in the job summary miss them
- status: CONFIRMED ISSUE
- priority: P2
- area: scripts/build-charts-data.js:249, 252, 260, 263; scripts/build-charts-intraday.js:159–160; scripts/build-pair-volume.mjs:107; scripts/fetch-coingecko-data.js:556; scripts/build-value-generated.mjs:875; detector scripts/pipeline-run.mjs:64
- problem: pipeline-run marks a step `carried` only when its stdout contains `::warning::` (`if (/::warning::/.test(l)) tail.carried = true`, line 64). `grep -c '::warning::'` over the scripts: dao-buys 1, holder-ranks 2, rank-history 2, every other builder 0. charts-data writes `{ ...prev[t.sym], carriedForward: true }` with a plain `console.warn('→ kept previous …')` and exits 0 as long as one token was fresh (267–270); charts-intraday the same per tier (159); pair-volume keeps a refused pool with `carried: prev.generatedAt` (107); coingecko keeps a figure with `carriedFrom` (546–561); value-generated keeps `burnPeriods` with `carriedForward: true` (875).
- evidence: the status file for this run says `"carried": 0` and every step `"carried": false`; the only three scripts that can set it are the three above. A PTGC chart series carried for three days shows ✅ success in the job summary, `lastSuccessAt` advancing every hour, and `carried: false`.
- why it matters: c20 was built precisely so that "a step that carries forward shows ⚠️, not ✅"; for 5 of the 8 steps that can carry, it does not. The owner's only signal is the page's own age label (charts.html `_carried` at 2013; index.html 12056/13498 for burnPeriods) — if he happens to open that surface.
- fix: one line at each of the five sites: `console.log('::warning::charts-data PTGC carried forward (' + reason + ')')` (same wording pattern as build-holder-ranks.mjs:114). No behaviour change; the summary shows ⚠️ and `counts.carried` becomes honest.
- effort: Small
- risk: Low
- recommendation: FIX NOW

#### D-3 · Nothing alerts when the pipeline stops being STARTED — and the dispatch token has an expiry date nobody recorded
- status: POTENTIAL RISK
- priority: P1
- area: handover/ptgcapi-worker-v7.js:108–131 (`dispatchDataPipeline`), .github/workflows/data-pipeline.yml:30–34, 72–84; HANDOVER.md:1658–1662 (token instructions: "a long expiry")
- problem: the hourly beat is the Worker's `7 * * * *` cron → `POST …/dispatches` with `DISPATCH_TOKEN` (a fine-grained PAT, default expiry 30 days, custom up to one year). On 401/403/404 the Worker does `console.error(...); return;` (126–127) — a Cloudflare log line nobody reads. GitHub's own `37 * * * *` schedule is "created only ~4 of these a day" (yml:31–33), so the site silently drops from 24 to ~4 runs a day. This already happened once for seven days (HANDOVER gotcha "Cloudflare cron triggers can sit unbound (2026-10-06)": "no scheduled events in 7 days … the daily sync included") and was found by looking, not by an alert. The 08:00 commission sync depends on a second PAT (`GITHUB_TOKEN`, ToolBox) with the same failure mode (`fetchReferralData` returns `[]` on any error → `success: true, 'No data to sync'`, worker 397–410).
- evidence: the dispatch has been live since 2026-10-07 00:07 (HANDOVER:556). If the PAT was created with the 30-day default it expires ~2026-11-05 — in two weeks; 90 days → ~2027-01-05; one year → ~2027-10-06. The notes do not say which. GitHub emails the token owner 7 days before expiry; that mail is the only reminder. GitHub's failed-workflow mail only fires for a run that EXISTS and goes red — a run that is never created sends nothing; a step that carries forward (D-2) exits 0 and sends nothing.
- why it matters: every tile's "as of" label goes amber at 2.5 h (c17) for every visitor while the owner sees nothing unless he opens the site; Audit III's whole hourly-cadence work (a29, c18) rests on one unmonitored token.
- fix: (1) today: GitHub → Settings → Developer settings → Fine-grained tokens → note DISPATCH_TOKEN's expiry in HANDOVER; if it is < 90 days, regenerate with a custom date ~1 year out (or no expiration) and paste it into the Worker secret. (2) the cheapest honest alert, no secrets: a second workflow `pipeline-watchdog.yml` on `schedule: '23 */3 * * *'` (GitHub creates it a few times a day even when delayed) whose one step does `curl -s https://raw.githubusercontent.com/ShakaVibe/PTGC-UFO-Dashboard/main/data/pipeline-status.json | node -e '…'` and exits 1 when `generatedAt` is > 3 h old or any hourly step's `lastSuccessAt` is > 6 h (2 h-gated > 8 h, rank-history > 48 h, carried > 24 h). A red run = GitHub's existing failure e-mail, within a few hours of a stall, and it covers the Worker being dead, the token being dead, and D-2's silent carries. (3) later, the Telegram version is c51.
- effort: Small
- risk: Low
- recommendation: FIX NOW

#### D-4 · Every pipeline script talks to ONE RPC (`rpc.pulsechain.com`); the pages rotate three
- status: POTENTIAL RISK
- priority: P2
- area: scripts/build-value-generated.mjs:15; scripts/fetch-burn-history.js:54; scripts/fetch-ufo-ptgc-burns.js:47; scripts/build-token-allocation.mjs:28; scripts/build-dao-buys.mjs:41; scripts/build-new-holders.mjs:37; scripts/build-swap-volume.mjs:40; scripts/build-rank-history.mjs:23; scripts/build-top-stakers.mjs:25 (`RPC_URLS`, never set in data-pipeline.yml)
- problem: `const RPC='https://rpc.pulsechain.com'` in eight scripts; only top-stakers has a pool (`(process.env.RPC_URLS || 'https://rpc.pulsechain.com').split(',')`) and the workflow does not set `RPC_URLS`. index.html:1825–1827 / portfolio.html:1090–1092 use three (`rpc.pulsechain.com`, `rpc-pulsechain.g4mm4.io`, `pulsechain-rpc.publicnode.com`) with the a41 rotate-on-429 logic.
- evidence: computed per hour from the code and the live files — value-generated: 12 chunked queries × ⌈751,337 blocks / 10,000⌉ = 76 ranges ≈ 912 `eth_getLogs` (+ halvings) in 398 s; top-stakers every 2 h: 5,138 `mapUserInfo` + 3,429 `balanceOf` = 8,567 `eth_call` in 32 s at 40-wide (≈270 calls/s); new-holders ≈ 250; rank-history ≈ 600/day; the rest < 50. ≈ 5,600 RPC calls an hour, ≈ 135k a day, all to one public endpoint. An outage or a rate-limit storm there fails the whole rpc lane and the holder-ranks lane's two RPC steps at once (honestly — every step carries or exits 1 — but all together).
- why it matters: it is the single largest external dependency of the hourly data and the only one with no fallback anywhere in `scripts/`. The 2026-09-20 a41 work solved exactly this in the browser.
- fix: a 10-line `rpc.mjs` helper (round-robin over `RPC_URLS`, skip an endpoint 30 s after a failure, as a41) imported by the five `.mjs` scripts that already have a small `rpc()` function (dao-buys, new-holders, swap-volume, rank-history, top-stakers: one-line import each); set `RPC_URLS` once in the yml `env`. The three "lifted from index.html" scripts (value-generated, burn-history, ufo-ptgc-burns) can take the same list into their existing `rpcFetch`/`rpcCall` with a modulo over attempts. Keep the public RPC first so the archive reads (new-holders' `balanceOf` at an old block, rank-history) stay on the node known to serve them.
- effort: Medium
- risk: Low
- recommendation: LATER

#### D-5 · `top-stakers` reads `balanceOf` for 3,429 wallets though only 110 are published, and re-reads 1,709 zero-stake wallets every run
- status: OPTIONAL IMPROVEMENT
- priority: P3
- area: scripts/build-top-stakers.mjs:87–97, 105–106
- problem: inside the 40-wide pool every known address gets `mapUserInfo` and, when staked, `balanceOf` (94). The output uses the balance only in `top: rows.slice(0, TOP_N).map(r => [r[0], key(r[1]), …, key(r[3])])` (105); `s`, `total`, `locked` use `r[1]`/`r[2]` only.
- evidence: live file: `count 3429`, cache `addrs 5138` → 5,138 + 3,429 = 8,567 `eth_call` per run, 12 runs a day = 103k calls/day — the pipeline's biggest burst (32 s). Reading `balanceOf` after the sort for the first 110 rows only: 3,429 → 110. Keeping a `zero: {addr: lastBlock}` map in the cache and re-reading a zero wallet only when a `Deposit` log named it since (the scan at 73–81 already yields exactly that set): 1,709 fewer. Together ≈ 3,540 calls/run (−59 %) with byte-identical output.
- why it matters: cuts the rpc load of D-4 by a third at no staleness cost; the 1 % `miss` guard (98) becomes less likely to trip on a rate-limited run.
- fix: move the `balanceOf` read to after `rows.sort(...)` for `rows.slice(0, TOP_N)`; add the zero map to the cache object written at 110.
- effort: Small
- risk: Low
- recommendation: LATER

#### D-6 · `rank-history` rewrites ~6,900 index files into `main`'s history every day (4,026 of 4,063 PTGC shards change per day)
- status: POTENTIAL RISK
- priority: P2
- area: scripts/build-rank-history.mjs:111–135 (`index()`: `fs.rmSync(dir, …)` then one file per shard), data-pipeline.yml:122 (`data/rank-index` in the Commit list)
- problem: every current holder gets one more delta per day, so almost every shard changes. Simulated on the live archive (`/home/claude/audit/work/D/idx-churn.mjs`: index with and without the last day): **4,026 of 4,063 PTGC shards differ, 31.3 MB rewritten**; UFO adds ~2,900. One daily commit therefore carries ~6,900 new blobs + a 4,064-entry tree + the 19 MB archive blob.
- evidence: `ls data/rank-index/PTGC | wc -l` = 4,064; the per-wallet entry is `[firstDayIndex, [delta per day]]` (122–129), extended to the wallet's last holding day. Git delta-compresses the push, so bytes are modest (the shards differ by a few characters each), but the OBJECT COUNT is ~2.5 M blobs a year on `main`, on top of D-6 (Audit III)'s 0.7 GB/yr. Every hourly checkout already pulls a 281 MB pack (`.git` of this depth-1 clone).
- why it matters: clone/fetch/gc time on the Mac and the runner grows with object count, not just bytes; c21's orphan `data` branch (still open) becomes the natural place for this — and its "squash yearly" is exactly what bounds it. The not-atomic `rmSync` + write (131–132) is the other half: a kill between them leaves a half-index, but `meta.json` is written last, so the PTGC gate re-runs the step next hour (UFO's dir is not gated — only `rank-index/PTGC/meta.json` is).
- fix: either (a) land c21 and commit `rank-history/` + `rank-index/` only on the data branch, squashed monthly; or (b) 256 shards (two hex chars) instead of 4,096 — 256 files/day (~120 KB each, ~40 KB gz per wallet lookup instead of ~8 KB), 16× fewer objects, one-line change in `a.slice(2, 5)` → `a.slice(2, 4)` plus the same in PfRank.jsx's shard name. (a) is the real fix; (b) is a stop-gap.
- effort: Medium
- risk: Low
- recommendation: LATER

#### D-7 · `swap-volume` prices the newest hour's trades from the PREVIOUS run's intraday file and never re-prices them
- status: LIKELY ISSUE
- priority: P3
- area: scripts/build-swap-volume.mjs:148–162 (`loadPrices` reads `data/charts-intraday.json`), 212–233 (USD fixed at ingestion); scripts/pipeline-run.mjs:45–48 (two lanes)
- problem: swap-volume is step 8 of the rpc lane (starts ~58 s into the run); charts-intraday is step 2 of the coingecko-key lane and lands after charts (135 s) + 70 s. The row's USD is `amt * p` with `p = prices[tk](ts)` = "this hour or up to 5 hours back" (157) and is stored in the cache row forever (`cache.swaps = rows`, 317).
- evidence: this run's stamps: `swap-volume.json` 21:09:30, `charts-intraday.json` 21:11:52 — 2 min 22 s later; the intraday file swap-volume read ended at hour 20:00 (its last point `2026-10-22T20:00Z` in the file written at 21:11; the one it actually read, from 20:11, ended at 19:00). So trades between 20:00 and 21:09 are valued at the 19:00 close, permanently. 26 rows this run, 0 unpriced — every one priced one or two hours stale.
- why it matters: the Humans vs Bots USD split is off by whatever the price moved in those hours; on a volatile hour that is a few percent on that hour's bar, baked in. Small, but it is a number users read and it never self-corrects.
- fix: at the start of each run re-price rows younger than 6 h (`rows.filter(r => now - r[0] < 6*HOUR)`) with the just-loaded `prices` — the amounts are in the row (r[6], r[8]); ~5 lines. (Moving the step to the other lane would cost the coingecko lane 4 s and keep the cross-lane read — not worth it.)
- effort: Small
- risk: Low
- recommendation: LATER

#### D-8 · `holder-history.json` keeps only 365 daily rows — the one record the chain cannot give back, and the c21 squash would erase the rest
- status: POTENTIAL RISK
- priority: P3
- area: scripts/fetch-burn-history.js:961–966 (`const maxSnapshots = 365; … slice(-maxSnapshots)`)
- problem: the only daily PulseScan holder count per token is trimmed to the last 365 days. Today: 275 rows since 2026-01-21 (27 KB, ~100 bytes a row). From 2027-01-21 the file forgets a day for every day it adds; the old rows then exist only in git history — which c21's plan ("squash yearly") deletes.
- evidence: readers use ≤ 90 days (index.html:8781 `holdersSeries(…days=30)`, 13373 `findClosest(90)`), so nothing breaks today; the loss is of the record, not of a display. A full year is ~37 KB.
- why it matters: "holders over the token's life" is a chart the owner will want one day (c55/c56 already lean on this file) and the data is unrecoverable once dropped.
- fix: delete the trim (or set it to 10 years); the file is 27 KB.
- effort: Small
- risk: Low
- recommendation: FIX NOW (one line)

#### D-9 · `rank-history`'s 20 h gate drifts 4 h earlier each cycle: every fifth run is a no-op that still rewrites the 19 MB archive, and a closed day can wait up to ~16 h
- status: OPTIONAL IMPROVEMENT
- priority: P3
- area: scripts/pipeline-run.mjs:42 (`gate: ['data/rank-index/PTGC/meta.json', 20]`); scripts/build-rank-history.mjs:82 (`yesterday`), 104–107 (`st.generatedAt = new Date().toISOString(); fs.writeFileSync(f + '.tmp' …)` on every run, extended or not), 133 (`meta.generatedAt`)
- problem: the stamp is the run time, so the step runs at 12:16 → 08:xx → 04:xx → 00:xx → 20:xx → 16:xx… A run at 20:07 finds `yesterday` already done ("already current"), still re-stamps the archive and rebuilds the index; the next run (16:07 next day) extends the day that closed at 00:00 sixteen hours late.
- evidence: live: archive `generatedAt 2026-10-22T12:12:40Z`, `cursorDay 2026-10-21`; status `rank-history … 9.0 h old (gate 20 h)`. The index rebuild is ~15 s of CPU per token (measured 29 s for two PTGC rebuilds in the simulation) plus `eth_blockNumber`; the archive rewrite is a 19 MB blob whose only change is the stamp.
- why it matters: minor cost, but the HOLDER RANK window's "days" lag by up to a day more than they need to, and a no-op commit touches the 19 MB file.
- fix: stamp `meta.generatedAt` (and the archive) with the close of `cursorDay` (`cursorDay + 'T00:00:00Z'` + 1 day) and gate at 1 h: the step then runs on the first hourly run after 00:45 UTC and extends exactly once a day; skip the archive write when `n === 0`.
- effort: Small
- risk: Low
- recommendation: LATER

#### D-10 · Platform budgets, with numbers (nothing is close today; two cliffs if the repo's visibility or traffic changes)
- status: POTENTIAL RISK
- priority: P3
- area: .github/workflows/data-pipeline.yml (24 runs/day), deploy.yml:55–60 (`_site`), index.html:1825–1827 and the 15 `raw.githubusercontent.com/…/main/data/` reads
- problem / evidence: (1) Actions minutes: ~461 s run + ~60 s checkout of a 281 MB pack + setup + commit ≈ 9–10 min × 24 ≈ 230 min/day ≈ 7,000 min/month — free only because the repo is public (Pages on the free plan needs it public anyway); flipping it private (e.g. to hide `handover/`) exhausts the 2,000 free minutes in ~9 days. (2) Pages: `_site` ≈ 3.2 MB pages + 16.8 MB logos + 7.8 MB data ≈ 28 MB (limit 1 GB) — fine; bandwidth soft limit 100 GB/month: a cold dashboard visit is 405 KB gz of index.html + art (logos/ is 17 MB on disk; c15 converted the big ones) ≈ 2–4 MB, a warm visit < 0.5 MB → ~30–50k cold visits/month before GitHub writes an e-mail. (3) raw.githubusercontent: a dashboard visit reads 2.34 MB raw / 556 KB gz of JSON (sum of the 15 files, measured) plus the 5-min quickRefresh; raw has no published bandwidth cap, only per-IP abuse limits, so 10× traffic is not a raw problem — but c39 (raw is the ONLY source; blocked by some corporate filters and in mainland China) is still open. (4) CoinGecko Pro key: coingecko 2×(3 + 2×20) + 5 = 91 calls + intraday 26 + pair-volume 49 + charts (every 2 h) ≈ 180/h ≈ 130k/month, at ≈ 1.5 calls/s across the two lanes that use it (pipeline-run.mjs:8 says the keyed scripts are serial; pair-volume is not, but the combined pace is far under the Analyst tier's 500/min). (5) PulseScan: holder-ranks 367 + 108 pages + 2 counters = 477 calls per 2 h (274 s, sequential, no pacing), treasury walks all 22 pages every hour, token-allocation/coingecko/burn-history a handful → ~270/h.
- why it matters: none of these bites now; the owner should know which knob is near a cliff (visibility, not traffic).
- fix: nothing to change; record the two cliffs in the HANDOVER's hardening section. If traffic does grow 10×, the free Cloudflare proxy in front of ptgc-ufo.com (the account already runs the Worker) caches the HTML/art and could serve `/data/` from a cached raw fetch — which also answers c39.
- effort: Small
- risk: Low
- recommendation: LATER

#### D-11 · Still open from Audit III (one line each, re-verified on the clone)
- c19 — `value-generated` still rescans the whole 90-day window every hour: 76 ranges × 12 queries ≈ 912 `eth_getLogs`, 398 s = 86 % of the run's wall time (now last in its lane, so nothing waits behind it; the checkpoint half is not done).
- c21 — orphan `data` branch not done; D-6 above raises the stakes.
- c22 — atomic writes done in 3 of 19 scripts only (fetch-coingecko-data, build-rank-history archive, pipeline-run); the cross-lane read (swap-volume ← charts-intraday) is D-7 above.
- c39 — every data read is raw.githubusercontent only; `_site/data/` is published daily and read by nobody.
- c42 — dead: `scripts/fetch-lv-data.js`, root `fetch-metrics.js`, root `holder-history.json` (1.8 KB, stamped 2026-01-12), `data/metrics-history.json` (123 KB, 2026-09-17, no reader), `data/lv-latest.json` (written at lv-snapshot.js:144, read by nobody), `liquidity-history.json` fetched by index.html with no consumer (21 KB gz per visit).
- D-11 (Audit III) — Worker: `DISPATCH_TOKEN || GITHUB_TOKEN` fallback, `CORS *`, non-constant-time compare, `/write` any path — unchanged in v7.

### Checked and found OK

- **Gates**: every gated file carries a stamp the gate understands (`lastUpdated` on charts-data / ufo-ptgc-burns / token-allocation, `generatedAt` on holder-ranks / top-stakers / rank-index meta, last element's `timestamp` on lv-snapshots) — verified on the live files; the 15-min tolerance keeps the 2 h / 6 h / 20 h steps on cadence; a missing/unreadable gate file → run (self-healing for a half-written rank-index, since meta.json is written last).
- **Missed days backfill**: `build-rank-history.mjs` extends `while (day <= yesterday)` (87) — two missed days = two days added, never skipped; `last >= head - 3` keeps a day's final block final; archive write is tmp+rename; any RPC failure aborts before the archive is written. Reflection factor from 6 untouched holders at both day ends is reproduced by the index deterministically. UTC throughout (`toISOString().slice(0,10)`, `getUTCMonth` for the burn periods, no `getDate()/getHours()` anywhere in `scripts/`).
- **Partial failure per step** (re-traced): burn-history throws on an unreadable chunk (a31) and writes nothing; treasury refuses a file with a hole (`assertReachedExisting`); new-holders exits before moving the cursor; swap-volume aborts on a refused chunk (cursor never skips) and rebuilds 90 d from scratch on a missing/old-schema cache (~100 s); top-stakers keeps the previous file on a failed log scan or > 1 % unread and rebuilds its address list from block 18,543,401 (~373 chunks at 12-wide, ~4 min) if the cache is gone; holder-ranks carries a token with `::warning::` and the 98 %-of-PulseScan-count check, exits 1 when both fail; token-allocation exits 1 when a token was kept; value-generated writes only when both scans are ok and the pre-window checkpoint (`ptgcPreWindow.toBlock 27114169`) advances each run (2 calls, not a rescan); coingecko appends no history point for a carried figure and stamps at save (c22); c4's time-based retention is in (853 points, 2026-08-16 → today, 100-day keep, hourly thinning after 7 d).
- **Commit list vs writers**: every path any script writes is in the yml's Commit list or the `data/ptgc-burns-*.json` glob (grep of all `data/…` literals in `scripts/`); nothing in the list is unwritten; `git add -A -- <paths>` stages deletions (the index `rmSync`, a retired period file). `[skip ci]` is redundant (GITHUB_TOKEN commits never trigger workflows) and harmless. `pull --rebase -X theirs` keeps THIS run's files on a data conflict (rebase semantics: "theirs" = the replayed bot commit) — correct.
- **Double runs**: the :37 fallback stands down when the last `generatedAt` is < 45 min old; the check runs when the queued run STARTS, so a schedule run queued behind a Worker dispatch also stands down; `concurrency: data-pipeline, cancel-in-progress: false` serialises and keeps at most one pending run (GitHub replaces an older pending run in the group with the newest — which is also why the empty "Redeploy" commit on 2026-10-22 overtook the stuck deploy: same group semantics in deploy.yml). `timeout-minutes: 30` is ~4× the 461 s run.
- **Status + report**: `pipeline-status.json` is tmp+rename, carries `lastSuccessAt`/`lastFailureAt`/`lastError` across skipped runs, `trigger` and `runUrl`; `--report` exits 1 on any failed step after the good files landed, and on a missing summary (a killed run) — both reach GitHub's failed-workflow e-mail to the token owner.
- **Repo/platform**: `_site` allow-list excludes the burn archive, the three caches, `rank-history/` and `rank-index/` (maxdepth 1); the largest period file is 47.3 MB and the current one grows ~33 MB per half-year (20.95 MB after 114 days) — the 100 MB file limit is not in reach with half-year periods; `data/` grows ≈ 70 MB/yr from the archive.
- **Caches that are safe to delete** (rebuild from the chain, no wrong numbers): `swap-volume-cache.json` (~100 s), `top-stakers-cache.json` (~4 min), `new-holders.json` (30-day backfill), `ufo-ptgc-burns-cache.json` (slow — see D-1 d). The `rank-history` archive is rebuildable with `design/portfolio/ranks-mock/backfill-fast.mjs` (hours, documented 2026-10-21). Not rebuildable: `holder-history.json`, `lv-snapshots.json`, the three CoinGecko histories (rolling 100 d), and `ToolBox/data/affiliate-commissions.json` (outside this repo, written only by the Worker).
- **Client-side error reporting**: there is none, and for a one-owner static site with the harness walk after every push that is the right call — a `window.onerror` POST would need a Worker endpoint and would mostly collect wallet-extension noise. The `ErrorBoundary` + Try again (a26) is the user-facing safety net; nothing to add.

### Ideas (grounded in what exists)

- **Watchdog as data, not only as e-mail**: once D-3's workflow exists, let it also write `data/pipeline-health.json` `{checkedAt, stale:[…]}` — the g4 freshness strip then has one 200-byte file to read, and the c51 Telegram digest can quote it.
- **Treasury: stop paging when a page is fully known** — the walk already asserts it reached the newest row on disk (`assertReachedExisting`); breaking out of the loop as soon as a page's every hash is already on disk turns 22 PulseScan pages an hour into 2–4 with identical files (the "heals old holes" property is kept by a weekly `--full` walk via `only=treasury` with an env flag).
- **value-generated checkpoint (c19), the smallest shape**: keep the decoded per-log rows (block, ts, amount, kind) for `[scanFrom, head − 1 day]` in an undeployed `value-generated-cache.json` (the swap-volume pattern at build-swap-volume.mjs:183–191) and rescan only the last day + the window's moving start: ~912 → ~20 `eth_getLogs`, 398 s → ~30 s, same numbers (the byPeriod sums are pure functions of the rows).
- **holder-ranks + rank-history share the 17k-row holder list**: the archive's `st.cur` (exact wei as of yesterday) × today's reflection factor is the same list PulseScan pages give — if the 2 h gate ever hurts, the people-only list can be derived from the archive once a day and the live `top` 110 read by `balanceOf` (110 calls instead of 477 pages). Not needed today; noted because both files already carry the data.
- **One `write-json.mjs`** (`writeFileSync(p + '.tmp'); renameSync`) imported by the 16 scripts that still write in place — c22's unfinished half; each script is a one-line change.
- **Record the token dates** in HANDOVER's "Account hardening" block: `DISPATCH_TOKEN` and the ToolBox `GITHUB_TOKEN` — created on, expires on, where it lives — so the 7-day GitHub mail is not the only memory.

## Raw sweep R

### Sweep R — Client-side blockchain / RPC / API usage and failure behaviour

Scope: index.html, portfolio.html (+ tools/pf-v2/*.jsx), calculators.html, charts.html, ledger.html, h2-header.jsx.
Method: code trace + a logging copy of the harness (`/home/claude/audit/work/R/run-r.js` = `tools/harness/run.js` with every
external request recorded as `<out>.reqs.json`, plus three extra switches: `LOGS_503=1` (eth_getLogs → HTTP 503, a transport
failure), `VG_OLD=<h>` (serve value-generated.json with `generatedAt` aged), `DATA_SLOW=<ms>` (delay only `data/*.json`)).
Every number below is from those runs (`out/*.reqs.json`, summarised with `summ.js`) unless marked "from code".
Harness caveat: the stub answers `/token-pairs/v1/` with an OBJECT, so index's 2.5 s hedge (`dsPairsFor`, 3643) fires two extra
DexScreener calls per token in the harness; the live endpoint answers an array, so "prod" DexScreener counts below are 1 per token.

### Calls per page load (measured, 1380×900, wait 8 s)

| Route | RPC posts (JSON-RPC calls) | by method | DexScreener | raw.githubusercontent | PulseScan | GeckoTerminal | other |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| `#/ptgc` | 31 (31) | eth_call 29, eth_getBalance 2 | 23 (prod ≈ 9) | 11 | 0 | 4 (2 are the 404 retry) | 7 logo PNGs |
| `#/ufo` | 32 (32) | eth_call 31, eth_getBalance 1 | 14 (prod ≈ 6) | 12 | 1 | 3 | 7 logos |
| `#/` Home | 20 (20) | eth_call 20 | 9 (prod ≈ 5) | 7 | 0 | 2 | — |
| calculators.html (PTGC) | 11 (11) | eth_call 11 | 7 (prod ≈ 4) | 1 | 0 | 0 | — |
| charts.html | 0 | — | 2 | 1 | 0 | 0 | 10 dd + 9 coingecko logos, 1 cdnjs |
| ledger.html | 0 | — | 0 | 1 | 0 | 0 | — |
| portfolio.html, 2 wallets (`HEAD_BLOCK=27100000 PF_STAKE=1 PF_TX=1`) | 43 (184) | eth_call 167 (112 history-chart balanceOf at past blocks in batches of 80/24/4, 16 for the 24 h tiles, 17 UFO eligible-supply batch, 13 per wallet single posts), eth_getBlockByNumber 7, eth_blockNumber 2, eth_getLogs 8 | 3 | 5 | 16 (4 transfer lists + 2 per detail row) | 0 | — |
| `#/ufo` with value-generated.json 5 h old (`VG_OLD=5`) | **991 (991)** | eth_getLogs 864, eth_getBlockByNumber 80, eth_call 45 | 14 | 12 | 1 | 4 | — |
| Live Feed open 61 s (`DECK_LOGS=8`) | +171 | eth_blockNumber 11 (one per 6 s), eth_getLogs 80 + eth_getBlockByNumber 80 (the one-off Top Pools 90-day scan) | — | — | — | — | — |

Where the `#/ptgc` 31 RPC calls go (from the trace): UFO boot 10 (factory 1, getPair 3, six fee getters — a PTGC visitor pays
them too, 1824–1958/17781), burn 2 (4043), hardcoded PTGC pairs DexScreener lacks (stub 6, prod 1 = PTGC/eHEX per
data/burn-summary.json), staking 2 (4597), DAO 14 (getBalance ×2, lockedAmount, proposalCount, 9 management-token balanceOf, PTGC
balance; + up to 11 `state()` reads when proposals exist, 4708). `#/ufo`: the six fee getters TWICE (R-7), PTGC chain-price seed
4 + PLS stable pools 4 (3399–3417), burn 2, maxSlippage/swapThreshold/contract balance/getBalance 4, seven pair balanceOf.
Per 5-min `quickRefresh` (measured after `click=button[aria-label^='Refresh']`): PTGC = burn 2 eth_call + 1 chain pair (eHEX) +
3 DexScreener (main pair, token-pairs, PLS) = 6 requests (≈ 72/h); UFO = burn 2 + up to 4 chain pairs + 3 DexScreener + 1
PulseScan `/counters` ≈ 10 (≈ 120/h). Besides it: new-holders.json (171 KB) and swap-volume.json every 5 min (c16, known),
coingecko-data.json every 15 min, GeckoTerminal candles every 30 min — all gated on `!document.hidden` except the Live Feed (R-12).

#### R-1 · Portfolio never starts reading wallets while a DexScreener quote hangs — three bare `fetch`es with no timeout gate the whole page
- status: CONFIRMED ISSUE
- priority: P1
- area: portfolio.html:1178–1186 (`quoteOf`), 1244–1251 (`usdOf`), 3641–3656 (the mount effect), 3659–3666 (wallet reads gated on `initialLoad`)
- problem: `const r=await fetch(`https://api.dexscreener.com/latest/dex/tokens/${addr}`); const d=await r.json();` (1180–1181) has no AbortController. `init()` awaits the three quotes, then `setInitialLoad(false)` (3653); the auto-fetch effect returns early `if(initialLoad||wallets.length===0)` (3660). index.html's `dsGet` gives every DexScreener call a 6–8 s timeout + 3 tries + a 15-min stale copy (3557–3618); the portfolio copy has none of it.
- evidence: `SLOW=40000 HEAD_BLOCK=27100000 HTML=portfolio.html … reload=20000` → at 20 s after reload the header reads `PTGC |   | UFO |   | PLS |  `, the wallet rows `A | ✎ | — | B | ✎ | —`, `MY WALLETS (2) · 2 counted · —`, and the request log holds NO `balanceOf` for 0x1111…/0x2222… — the only RPC traffic is the 17-call eligible-supply batch and one `eth_getBlockByNumber` (`out/pf-hang.reqs.json`). A 9 s delay (`SLOW=9000`) costs exactly 9 s of blank page. The Aug-2026 incident in the HANDOVER was "15 s hangs per endpoint"; a stalled DexScreener connection has no browser-side limit short of the network stack's own (minutes).
- why it matters: the one page where visitors look at their own money shows an empty shell with no error and no Retry for as long as DexScreener stalls, although the chain (balances, staking, locks) is reachable and USD is only a multiplier the page already renders as "—" when null.
- fix: give `quoteOf`/`usdOf` an 8 s AbortController (copy `_dsOnce`'s shape from index 3569–3596), and do not gate wallet reads on prices: fire `fetchWalletData` for saved wallets on mount and let `prices` land whenever they land (the tiles already print "—" for a null price via `usd()` 1255). Smallest change: `setInitialLoad(false)` before `await Promise.all(...)`.
- effort: Small
- risk: Low
- recommendation: FIX NOW

#### R-2 · PTGC dashboard first paint waits on six raw.githubusercontent reads that have no timeout; the boot retry ladder cannot help because `loading` never clears
- status: CONFIRMED ISSUE
- priority: P1
- area: index.html:13442–13462 (`cachesP` awaited before `fetchDex` for PTGC), 4157–4175 / 4441–4455 / 4481–4532 (the six readers: bare `fetch(URL,DATA_FETCH)`, no AbortController), 13806–13819 (ladder: `if(loading)return;`)
- problem: `if(token==='UFO'){…}else{ await cachesP; … setBurnPeriods(getBurnPeriods(token)); }` (13454–13460) runs BEFORE `const[d,h,b,p]=await Promise.all([fetchDex…])` (13462). `cachesP` is `Promise.all([fetchBurnHistory(),fetchCoingeckoData(),fetchLiquidityHistory(),fetchTransactionHistory(),fetchHolderHistoryNew(),fetchTokensInLPHistory()])` — six downloads (transaction-history.json 338 KB, burn-summary 138 KB, liquidity-history 103 KB, tokensinlp 97 KB …) whose `fetch` calls carry no signal. Only the burn-period seed needs them, and `setBurnPeriods` could be called when they land.
- evidence: `DATA_SLOW=30000 node run-r.js "#/ptgc" … wait=12000` → at ~14.5 s the page is still the skeleton (`MARKET CAP | 30D | VOLUME 24H | i | 30D | LIQUIDITY | RH | 30D …`, no price, `rpc 7` = the UFO boot only, no DexScreener call issued); the same run on `#/ufo` has painted `$0.000142 | +3.21% | MARKET CAP | $42,600,000 …` because UFO skips the await (13454). A hung CDN (c39 describes blocked ones; a stalled one is the same from the browser's side) leaves `loading=true`, so `loadError` is never set (13464 is after the gate) and the ladder returns at 13807 — skeleton forever, no Retry.
- why it matters: PTGC is the landing dashboard; every visitor's first paint is bounded by the slowest of six static files on a third-party CDN instead of by the price fetch, and the honest-failure design (error card + Retry) cannot trigger.
- fix: move the `await cachesP` + `setBurnPeriods(getBurnPeriods(token))` into a `.then` (or after first paint, where UFO already awaits it at 13536) so `fetchDex`/`fetchBurn` start at once; and/or wrap the six readers in one `fetchData(url)` helper with an 8 s AbortController (same shape as `_dsOnce`). Either alone fixes the hang; both make PTGC paint as fast as UFO.
- effort: Small
- risk: Low
- recommendation: FIX NOW

#### R-3 · Calculators' full-screen gate hangs on a DexScreener or raw.githubusercontent stall — bare `fetch`es in `fetchDex` and `fetchCoingeckoData`
- status: CONFIRMED ISSUE
- priority: P1
- area: calculators.html:5009–5018 (`load`: `await fetchCoingeckoData()` then `Promise.all([fetchDex…])`), 456 (`fetch(COINGECKO_DATA_URL,…)`), 307–310 (`Promise.all([fetch(…/latest/dex/tokens/…),fetch(…/token-pairs/v1/…)])`), 638–639 (same in `lvFetchTokenPairs`), 2888, 479 (GeckoTerminal), 5116 (`if(loading&&!loadedOnce)` = full-screen "Loading Calculators…")
- problem: the page's own `dsJson` (249–261) has an 8 s timeout and is used for the main pair and PLS, but the two token endpoints inside `fetchDex` and the coingecko file are bare `fetch`. The first load is gated on all of them.
- evidence: `SLOW=40000 HTML=calculators.html node run-r.js "" … wait=20000` → body text at ~22 s is `Loading Calculators... | Fetching PTGC data`; request log: 6 DexScreener requests outstanding, 3 RPC posts, nothing else. (`SLOW=9000` = a 9 s blank page.)
- why it matters: same hang class as R-1 on the page people use to size a buy; no error card, no retry, and a token Switch cannot help because `loadedOnce` is still false.
- fix: use `dsJson` for lines 308–309 and 638–639 (it already handles 429/5xx/non-JSON), give `fetchCoingeckoData` an 8 s AbortController, and do not `await fetchCoingeckoData()` before the price round (it is only read by the volume windows at 5057–5065).
- effort: Small
- risk: Low
- recommendation: FIX NOW

#### R-4 · A stale value-generated.json turns every UFO visit into ~950 RPC calls (864 eth_getLogs) — 312 of them on pools the contract never mints into
- status: POTENTIAL RISK
- priority: P1
- area: index.html:4061 (`VALUE_GEN_STALE_MS=3h`), 13087–13095 (`fetchDeliveredValue(…,HARDCODED_UFO_PAIRS)`), 2620–2680 (four Transfer scans + a Mint scan per pair), 1720–1728 (seven pairs since 2026-10-06), 1947 (the three constructor pools), 1909–1916 (`UFO_FEE_BUCKETS` plsLP/plsxLP/wethLP); scripts/build-value-generated.mjs:837–842 (the pipeline scans 4 pairs)
- problem: when the hourly file is older than 3 h the dashboard re-derives the UFO windows in the browser. Measured with `VG_OLD=5` on `#/ufo`: **991 RPC posts** in ~3.3 s — `eth_getLogs 864` = UFO Transfer ×236 (three scans: out-of-contract, to-burn, fee-in), PTGC Transfer ×80, and `[[Mint,Mint(pulsex)]]` on SEVEN pairs ×78 each = 546 — plus 80 `eth_getBlockByNumber` (boundary searches). The contract's auto-liquidity only ever mints into the WPLS / PLSX / WETH pools (fee buckets 1913–1915; `resolveUfoPairs` 1947 filters exactly those three), so the Mint scans on UFO/INC, UFO/HEX, UFO/PRVX, UFO/eHEX (added 2026-10-06 "so a pool DexScreener drops still shows") are 312 calls whose logs are discarded by the swapback-tx intersection (2755: `origin===contract` or `swapbackTxs[tx]`). The scans grow with the token's age until the 90-day window caps them (~78 chunks today).
- evidence: `out/ufo-vgold.reqs.json` (breakdown by address/topic: `{'0x49eD…Transfer':236,'0x94534…Transfer':80, 7 pairs × 78}`); the 2026-10-01 note at 13488 says the chain path used to cost "~55 eth_getLogs for four numbers" — it is 864 now because the Mint pairs went 3 → 7 and the window is a full 90 days. c17/c20 record pipeline gaps of 2–5 h and a "stalled builder" (4062); the Worker cron also sat unbound for 7 days (HANDOVER 1590).
- why it matters: the fallback fires per VISITOR, in CONC=3 bursts, against a public node that "throttles bursts" (1811): ten viewers during a pipeline stall = ~9,000 JSON-RPC calls in half a minute → -32005 → every endpoint in cooldown (1839–1862) → "chain read failed" for everyone, including the PTGC page's burn/holder reads that share the pool. The pipeline itself (c19) rescans the same window hourly for the same reason.
- fix: (1) pass only the three constructor pools to `fetchDeliveredValue` (`HARDCODED_UFO_PAIRS.filter(p=>['WPLS','PLSX','WETH'].includes(p.quoteSymbol))`, the 1947 filter) — 864 → 552 with identical numbers (no contract mint can exist on the other four); the pipeline builder scans 4 (eHEX included) — align both on the same list. (2) Keep painting the stale file (it already does, 13497–13503) but defer the browser rescan behind the existing Retry/"as of" affordance, or raise the trigger from 3 h to the 48 h interim limit (4067) so a normal pipeline hiccup does not fan out to every visitor. If (2) touches a financial display, it changes only WHEN the live scan runs, not any formula.
- effort: Small (1) / Medium (2)
- risk: Low (1) / Medium (2)
- recommendation: FIX NOW (1), LATER (2)

#### R-5 · `rpcFetch` / `getLogsRange` drift: portfolio and calculators carry the pre-a41 pool — no cooldown, no -32005 rotation, and the log scan bisects on a dead transport (7× the calls, up to 7 min per range)
- status: CONFIRMED ISSUE
- priority: P2
- area: portfolio.html:1111–1125 (`rpcFetch`), 1412–1439 (`getLogsRange`), 1450–1464; calculators.html:228–242 (`rpcFetch`); vs index.html:1839–1890 (a41) and 2066–2101
- problem: index's pool remembers a failed endpoint for 30 s (`RPC_COOLDOWN_MS`, 1839–1862), treats a JSON-RPC `-32005` / "rate limit" answer as a transport failure and rotates (1870–1871), and returns `{result:null,transport:true}` so `getLogsRange` fails fast instead of halving (2085–2089). Both sibling copies still return a bare `{result:null}` (1124, 241); portfolio's `getLogsRange` then halves the range `if(depth<6&&(b-a)>250)` (1428) exactly as index did before a41, and a `-32005` on a batch-happy node is bisected too (the thing a41 says "only multiplies requests against a node that is already throttling").
- evidence: `LOGS_503=1 HEAD_BLOCK=27100000 HTML=portfolio.html …` (2 wallets) → **168 eth_getLogs HTTP posts** (`rpc.pulsechain.com 80, g4mm4 56, publicnode 56`) = 56 logical calls = 8 ranges (2 wallets × 2 directions × 2 chunks) × 7 (depth 0→6 down the left branch, since `if(left===null)return null`); index's logic stops at depth 0: 8 × 3 endpoints = 24 posts. With a HANGING node (the Aug-2026 pattern; 20 s timeout at 1415) that is 7 × 3 × 20 s = 7 min per range; today's live span is 27,865,507 − 26,989,200 = 876k blocks = 9 chunks per direction, CONC=3 → 3 rounds × 7 min ≈ 21 min per wallet before "unavailable — Retry", and the queue runs one wallet at a time (1703–1719): five wallets ≈ 1 h 45 min of a spinning "scanning the chain…". Calculators' copy only does single `eth_call`s (8 s × 3 per call) — the `-32005` gap there means a rate-limited answer is handed to `rpcWord` as a real error (→ null, honest) instead of trying the next endpoint.
- why it matters: the two pages that read the chain per wallet hit the pool hardest and have the weakest copy of it; the HANDOVER already recorded the exact failure once ("up to ~7 minutes of retries per scan, three scans deep", 1885–1888).
- fix: copy index 1839–1890 verbatim into both pages (same three endpoints, same names), and in portfolio's `getLogsRange` add the two a41 lines (`if(r&&r.transport){lastLogError=…;return null;}` before the halving). No formula touched.
- effort: Small
- risk: Low
- recommendation: FIX NOW

#### R-6 · DAO Treasury totals are printed as fact when one of their fifteen reads fails — a missing PTGC price silently drops ~$35.9K from "Total DAO Value"
- status: LIKELY ISSUE
- priority: P1
- area: index.html:4676–4820 (`fetchDAOData`), specifically 4728–4729 (`BigInt(mgmtBalanceResult.result||'0x0')`), 4739–4743 / 4662–4671 (`fetchTokenPrice` → `0` on failure), 4753–4778 (`if(tokenResult.result&&…)` else the token is skipped; `if(t&&t.usdValue>0)` drops a priced-at-0 token), 4784–4792 (`ptgcPrice=ptgcPriceRes||0`), render 14786 / 14803 / 14838 / 14867 / 14872 (`fmtUSD(daoData.totalValue||0)` etc.)
- problem: a16 fixed only step 1 (the DAO contract balance → `null`). Every later read degrades to 0: the management wallet's PLS balance, each of nine token balances, each of six DexScreener prices (`dsPairsFor` → `[]` → `dsPriceOf` null → `|| 0`), and the treasury's PTGC price. A token with a real balance and a failed price is removed from `managementTokens` entirely (4774), so the panel's list and both totals shrink with no "—", no "partial" flag.
- evidence: worked example with today's files — data/treasury-summary.json wallet1 (`0xeeac1da7…`): PTGC 1,999,973,694.37, HEX 13,257,855.02, native PLS 3,148,866,225.41; data/charts-data.json last closes PTGC $0.0000179530, HEX $0.0012417227, WPLS $0.0000054649. PTGC leg = 1,999,973,694 × 0.000017953 = **$35,906**; HEX leg = 13,257,855 × 0.0012417 = **$16,462**; PLS leg = 3,148,866,225 × 0.0000054649 = **$17,208**. One failed `fetchTokenPrice(TOKENS.PTGC.address)` (a 429 burst — the DAO panel fires six `dsPairsFor` calls in parallel at 4739–4744, right after the dashboard's own) prints a Total DAO Value $35.9K lower, in the same gold type, same "ⓘ", no label. `rpcFetch` resolving `{result:null}` for the management wallet's `eth_getBalance` prints it $17.2K lower. Not harness-demonstrated (the stub cannot fail one read selectively); traced line by line.
- why it matters: gotcha 20's rule ("a read that failed is null, never 0") is applied to the headline only; the figure people quote in the community ("DAO is worth $X") can be tens of thousands low on a flaky minute and looks identical to a good read.
- fix: in `fetchDAOData` carry `partial:true` whenever any balance or price read returned null/0 (keep the per-token `price:null`), and in the render print "—" for `managementValue` / `totalValue` (or "≥ $X · n reads failed") when `partial`; the a18 `CardLoadState` + Retry pattern already exists for second-fetch cards. No formula change — only which reads are allowed to print.
- effort: Small
- risk: Low
- recommendation: FIX NOW

#### R-7 · The six UFO fee getters are read twice on every UFO visit (boot + load), and every PTGC / Home visit pays the UFO boot's ten eth_calls
- status: OPTIONAL IMPROVEMENT
- priority: P3
- area: index.html:17781 (`bootReady` → `bootstrapUFO` → `fetchUfoFees`), 13651–13653 (`load()` for UFO → `fetchUfoFees(cfg.address)` again), 13425 (`await bootReady` just before)
- problem: `bootstrapUFO` already wrote `ufo.feeRate` / `ufo.feeBreakdown` (17770–17776); `load()` awaits that very promise and then re-reads the same six `public` getters 0.45 s later. Trace on `#/ufo`: `0x83ad7994 … 0xd5d58ce7` at t=3257–3269 ms and again at t=3709–3739 ms (`out/ufo.reqs.json`). `quickRefresh` never calls it, so the comment "re-read live fees each refresh" (13650) is not what happens; the second read only matters on a later token switch.
- evidence: six duplicate `eth_call`s per UFO page load, same method + params, 450 ms apart. On `#/ptgc` and Home the boot's 10 eth_calls (factory, 3 getPair, 6 fees) run too although nothing on those routes reads them.
- why it matters: 6–10 wasted calls per visit against the pool everything else shares; small, but free to remove.
- fix: in `load()` skip `fetchUfoFees` when `bootReady` resolved with fees in the last few minutes (a `ufoFeesAt` stamp set in `bootstrapUFO`); keep the read on token switch.
- effort: Small
- risk: Low
- recommendation: LATER

#### R-8 · Portfolio: per-wallet reads are 7 + 2×locks single POSTs per wallet, all wallets at once; the history chart batches 80 against a node where "batches of 100 failed, 40 worked"
- status: POTENTIAL RISK
- priority: P2
- area: portfolio.html:1596–1604 / 1636–1651 / 1661 (`callContract` ×6, ×2 per lock, ×1), 3661–3665 (`wallets.forEach(fetchWalletData)`), 1999 (`PFH_BATCH=80`), 2086–2094 (two workers), 2088–2090 (a failed batch → `res=null` → every point in it stays unread); HANDOVER.md:177
- problem: a 20-wallet portfolio fires ≥140 concurrent single-call POSTs at `rpc.pulsechain.com` on mount (measured 13 posts per wallet with 3 locks), the burst pattern index.html's pool comment names as what triggers throttling; the page already knows how to batch (`pfhRpc` sends arrays, 2088) but uses it only for history. The history batch size 80 sits between the two values the owner measured (100 failed / 40 worked) for the same node; when a batch is refused, `run` swallows it and the chart shows a GAP (honest, but silent, and the whole 80-point batch is lost, not one point).
- evidence: `out/pf.reqs.json` — batches `17@10391 6@10775 16@11022 80@11435 24@11436 4@11436`; per wallet at t=10780–10925: 6 PTGC calls + 6 lock calls as 12 separate posts, both wallets interleaved. ROADMAP E7 lists "`80` batch" as an unnamed magic number.
- why it matters: a rate-limited mount marks wallets "Read failed" (a11 — honest) and a refused batch blanks a slice of the value chart; both are avoidable with the batching the page already has.
- fix: send the six PTGC calls of a wallet as ONE batch (and the lock pairs as one), cap wallet concurrency at ~3; set `PFH_BATCH` to 40 (the measured-good size) or halve a refused batch once before giving up.
- effort: Small
- risk: Low
- recommendation: LATER

#### R-9 · Live Feed polling has no hidden-tab guard; ~1,300 RPC calls per hour per open deck, and the first open costs 160 calls
- status: OPTIONAL IMPROVEMENT
- priority: P2
- area: index.html:5018 (`DECK_POLL_MS=6000`), 5546–5579 (`tick`: `eth_blockNumber` each tick, `fetchBoth` = 2 `eth_getLogs` when the head moved, 5464–5468), 5439–5441 (`eth_getTransactionReceipt` per fee-leg tx, cap 40), 5052–5110 (`fetchDeckPoolVolumes`: the 90-day Top Pools scan)
- problem: the 5-min refresh (13821), the NH re-read (10669), the H2 candles (9882) and the Home spark (6164) all check `document.hidden`; the deck's `setTimeout(tick,…)` chain does not, so a Live Feed left open in a background tab keeps polling (Chrome throttles chained timers to ~1/min after 5 min hidden; Firefox/Safari differ — but nothing in the page decides).
- evidence: `DECK_LOGS=8 … click=Live Feed; wait=60000` → after the click: `eth_blockNumber 11` in 61 s (one per 6 s; the stub head never moves so no per-block getLogs were issued) plus `eth_getLogs 80 + eth_getBlockByNumber 80` for the one-off Top Pools scan (incremental afterwards via `LS.DECK_POOLS`). From code, with ~10.3 s blocks: 600 `eth_blockNumber` + ~700 `eth_getLogs` (2 × ~350 head advances) + receipts ≈ 1,300–1,500 calls/h per viewer with the deck open.
- why it matters: a dozen community members leaving the deck open all day is a steady ~15–18k calls/h on the public pool that the dashboard's burn/holders reads and the portfolio's scans depend on.
- fix: `if(document.hidden){timer=setTimeout(tick,DECK_POLL_MS*5);return;}` at the top of `tick` plus a `visibilitychange` resume (same shape as 13822); optionally seed Top Pools from data/swap-volume.json (`pools` per-hour rows are already there) instead of the 80-chunk scan.
- effort: Small
- risk: Low
- recommendation: LATER

#### R-10 · calculators.html and ledger.html mount with no ErrorBoundary — a render throw is a blank page
- status: POTENTIAL RISK
- priority: P2
- area: calculators.html:5263 (`ReactDOM.createRoot(...).render(<CalculatorsPage/>)`), ledger.html:1510; vs index.html:17617 (`ErrorBoundary`, a26) and portfolio.html:4045 (`PortfolioBoundary`)
- problem: React 18 unmounts the whole root on an uncaught render error. Audit II G (a10) documented exactly this on the portfolio ("a malformed saved-wallet entry blanks portfolio.html permanently") and it got a boundary; the calculators page — which renders user-typed numbers through dozens of formatters — and the ledger did not.
- evidence: `grep -c ErrorBoundary calculators.html ledger.html` → 0 / 0; `grep "extends React.Component"` → only portfolio.
- why it matters: one `toFixed` on an unexpected `undefined` in a less-travelled tab is a black page with no Back button for every visitor until a deploy.
- fix: copy portfolio's `PortfolioBoundary` (4045–4067, ~20 lines: card + Reload + Back) around `<CalculatorsPage/>` and `<App/>`.
- effort: Small
- risk: Low
- recommendation: LATER

#### R-11 · Helper drift on the sibling pages: `fmtAbbr` prints "1000.00K", `getBurnC` undercounts at exact tenths — both fixed in index.html, not in the copies
- status: CONFIRMED ISSUE
- priority: P3
- area: portfolio.html:1129 and calculators.html:290 (`fmtAbbr`, old `>=1e3` thresholds) vs index.html:3406 (`>=999.995e3`, Audit II A); calculators.html:298 (`getBurnC` with `Math.floor(rem/c.p)`) vs index.html:3393–3398 + 3485 (`creatureCount`, a42); caller calculators.html:4529–4533 (Fresh Capital creature row)
- problem: the two "same" helpers have different bodies. `fmtAbbr(999999)` → `"1000.00K"` on portfolio/calculators, `"1.00M"` on index; `fmtAbbr(999999999)` → `"1000.00M"`. `getBurnC(0.3)` on calculators → `Shark×2 Dolphin×9 Squid×9 Turtle×9 Shrimp×9` (shown sliced to 4) because `0.3/0.1 = 2.9999999999999996`; index prints `Shark×3`. 38 of the 100 exact tenths between 0.1 % and 10 % were wrong before a42 (index comment 3385–3391).
- evidence: `node -e` with the two bodies (see examples above); `drift.js` in `/home/claude/audit/work/R/` lists every name defined in 2+ files with differing bodies.
- why it matters: a holder with 999.9995M PTGC sees "1000.00M" on the portfolio and "1.00B" on the dashboard; a Fresh Capital scenario that lands on an exact tenth shows one creature too few plus a tail of ×9s.
- fix: paste index's two bodies (and `creatureCount`) into both pages. Pure formatting / counting; the underlying numbers are untouched.
- effort: Small
- risk: Low
- recommendation: LATER

#### R-12 · Address tables that drifted or point at the retired world (none change a live number today)
- status: POTENTIAL RISK
- priority: P3
- area: calculators.html:148–153 (`HARDCODED_UFO_PAIRS` = 4 pairs, `WETH … isRHCore: true`) vs index.html:1720–1728 (7 pairs, WETH `isRHCore: false` "must stay out of the RH modal"); calculators.html:575–578 (`LV_RH_CORE_PAIRS` holds `0xbea0e55b…` "UFO/PRVC" = the RETIRED contract's PRVX pair — scripts/fetch-burn-history.js:39 `UFO_LP_PAIR`); ledger.html:51 (`KNOWN_TOKENS` maps only the old UFO `0x456548…`, not `0x49eD…`), 81–86 (`KNOWN_ROUTERS`: `0x165c3410fc91b0e65d87e89d8bcadd85c9e6dbf1` and `0x98bf93ebf5c380c0e6ae8e192a7e2ae08edacc3a` are NOT the routers the treasury trades with — data/treasury-recent.json shows 9 txs to `0x165c3410fc91ef562c50559f7d2289febed552d9` (= `ADDR.PULSEX_ROUTER`) and 6 to `0x98bf93ebf5c380c0e6ae8e192a7e2ae08edacc02`), same two wrong strings in scripts/fetch-treasury-transactions.js:76–80; index.html:1646–1648 (`daoContract`/`daoTreasury`/`stakingContract` literals in `TOKENS`, not `ADDR` — gotcha 00) and 12491–12494 (`T1_EXCL` repeats them + the UFO OA wallet `0x92dd8e86…`, also a literal in portfolio.html:1829 and PfLeagues.jsx:23)
- problem: none of these is on a live path that produces a wrong figure: `isRouter` (ledger 435) is computed and never read; the `0xbea0…` set entry can never match a new-contract pair (the symbol fallback at 629 does the work); calculators' chain-read UFO pairs are only reached when DexScreener lacks the pair (and WETH's flag only feeds `_rhCore` there). They are, however, the next migration's trap: a fifth person adding a pool will find three different UFO pair lists (index 7, calculators 4, build-value-generated.mjs 4, PF_UFO_EXCL 13).
- evidence: `grep -oiE '0x[0-9a-f]{40}'` per file vs the `ADDR` block: index 3 real literals outside `ADDR` (+T1_EXCL), portfolio 10, calculators 6, charts 4 (pair addresses for PLSX/HEX/eHEX/INC — correct), ledger 9.
- why it matters: low today; the ledger's two wrong router strings are the kind of thing that gets copied into a classifier later.
- fix: one line each — fix/delete the two ledger router strings (and the script's), add `UFO_PRVX_V1_PAIR` to a comment or drop the set entry, map `ADDR.UFO` in the ledger's `KNOWN_TOKENS`, move the three `TOKENS` literals into `ADDR`, and have calculators import index's pair list by copy (or at least the same 7 + flags).
- effort: Small
- risk: Low
- recommendation: LATER

#### R-13 · Failure cues that stop short: "+0.00%" on charts' header when DexScreener omits `priceChange`; portfolio's "waiting for your transactions…" after PulseScan failed
- status: LIKELY ISSUE
- priority: P3
- area: charts.html:665 (`const chg = p.priceChange?.h24 ? parseFloat(p.priceChange.h24) : 0;`), 2544–2548; tools/pf-v2/PortfolioV2.jsx:460 / portfolio.html:3507 (`r7.state==='ok'?'waiting for your transactions…'`), tools/pf-v2/PfTx.jsx:301 (the "Couldn't reach PulseScan right now · Retry" line lives inside the collapsed Recent Transactions fold)
- problem: (a) a stale-but-valid DexScreener answer without `priceChange` (what a quiet or chain-read pair sends) prints "+0.00%" on charts.html where index.html and the v2 header print "—" (`changeKnown` h2-header.jsx:116). (b) With PulseScan down the portfolio's UFO tile line says "waiting for your transactions…" indefinitely and the TRADES strip is simply empty; the only honest message is behind "SHOW ▾".
- evidence: `PS_DOWN=1 … portfolio` screenshot (`out/pf-psdown2.png`): `🛸 Reflections today  waiting for your transactions…`, empty strip, fold collapsed; `DS_DOWN=1 HTML=charts.html` prints "—" (the `!r.ok` return) so only the omitted-field case is affected — from code.
- why it matters: small, but both are the "0 is a claim" rule (gotcha 20) applied inconsistently.
- fix: charts 665 → `null` and let `h2ApplyValues` print "—"; PortfolioV2.jsx 460 → when `txs&&txs.failed===txs.total` say "PulseScan unreachable — Retry" (the fold's own state).
- effort: Small
- risk: Low
- recommendation: LATER

### Checked and found OK (do not touch)

- index.html's RPC pool (1824–1890): three endpoints, 8 s `eth_call` / 20 s `eth_getLogs` timeouts via AbortController, sticky 2 min, a41 cooldown + `-32005` rotation + `transport` flag; `getHeadBlock` shares one `eth_blockNumber` per 4 s (2022–2030) — measured 1 per load; `getLogsChunked` memoises in-flight scans per 5-min bucket (the UFO burn-period scan and `fetchDeliveredValue`'s to-burn scan ran ONCE each in the `VG_OLD=5` run, not twice — Audit II D's double fee effect is contained by the dedupe).
- `dsGet` (3557–3618): in-flight dedupe, 30 s fresh cache, 4-concurrent / 90 ms throttle, 3 tries honouring `Retry-After`, 15-min stale fallback; `dsPairsFor` hedges instead of firing three generations (measured: 1 call per token when the primary answers an array). `fetchDex` keeps `change/vol/buys/sells` null when every pair is chain-read (a14) — the `DS_DOWN` runs printed "—" for all four on both dashboards and the LIQUIDITY tile carried "chain · core pools only".
- Both-down path: the error card with Retry appeared on `#/ptgc` and `#/ufo`; Home showed "— / refresh failed" and the ladder issued its rungs (81/112/114 posts in ~18 s = 4 s + 10 s rungs with 3 endpoints each) — a51 holds.
- `fetchHolders` (3993–4027): file-first for PTGC, PulseScan 5 s + one retry, returns `null` never 0; `PS_DOWN=1` kept 18,200 / 5,325 from the files.
- `fetchBurn` (4043) and `fetchStakingData` (4597) return `null` on any missing word; the Burn panel, Leagues and allocation printed "—" under `RPC_DOWN=1`; `burn` state is seeded `{total:0,…supply:0}` but every reader checks `supply>0` (`burnKnown` 11764, 11917, 12351, 13928) — not a claim.
- Live Feed tick backs off exponentially to 60 s on a dead head (5554–5557); under `LOGS_503=1` the deck issued 10 getLogs posts in 20 s and degraded to `partial` / Top Pools `failed` (5544, 5527) — no bisect storm, because it calls `getLogsRange` at depth 0 and the a41 transport check returns null at once.
- GeckoTerminal: `useH2Series` 720-candle read is cached 30 min in `LS.H2_PX` (one call per token per load, measured), the LP curves read data/pair-volume-7d.json first (all 7 UFO pools and 49 pools are in the file — only the stub's fake pair went live), the per-pool queue is 1.1 s apart; `fetchHomeSpark` / `fetchH2Price7d` / `fetchPoolVol7d` all carry 8 s AbortControllers (6126, 9809, 10368).
- Visibility guards: the 5-min `quickRefresh` (13821, 6378), `fetchNewHolders` (10669), the H2 candles (9882), the Home spark (6164) all skip while `document.hidden`; `onVis` refreshes once after >5 min away.
- Address comparisons: every `===` / `Set.has` on addresses I could find lowercases both sides (`pairSet` 3085/5419 from `lc`, `T1_EXCL` lower-case literals, portfolio `PFT_FEE` lower-case, `pricePair`/`dsPricePair` `String(..).toLowerCase()`); no mixed-case miss found. Explorer links use `scan.pulsechain.com/{tx,address,token}/`, `dexscreener.com/pulsechain/{pair}`, `dextools.io/app/en/pulse/pair-explorer/{pair}` — all correct for chain 369. switch.win gets `network:'pulsechain'` (1685). Portfolio wallet input is `/^0x[0-9a-fA-F]{40}$/` + duplicate check with a clear message (3258–3259); `sanitizeWallets` drops bad saved entries (a10).
- localStorage: index's `LS` table is versioned and read through `lsGet(key,shapeOk)` (tiers 13592–13595, H2_PX, LP_VOL, DECK_POOLS); portfolio's `grays_ufo_refl_v1:<addr>` is shape-checked (`o.v===1`, integer `to` within launch…head, 1489) and discarded on a negative result; `grays_pf_hist_v1` prunes >400 d and 6-h points >15 d and sheds the oldest tenth on quota; `grays_pf_tx_v4:` is one key per wallet+token with LRU eviction on quota; charts' `sessionStorage hdr_*` is 10 min. `grays_pf_txd_v2` (tx details) is never pruned but only grows by rows that were on screen.
- Portfolio reflections queue (a12): one wallet at a time, `gen`-guarded; `LOGS_DOWN=1` ended "unavailable — chain read failed" with Retry, never 0 (and the B8 incremental cache is only written on success, 1508).
- No `Promise.all` in a load path can be killed by one rejection: every leg catches internally (`fetchDex`/`fetchHolders`/`fetchBurn`/`fetchPLS` in index; `quoteOf` in portfolio; `fetchDex`/`fetchBurn`/`fetchPLS` in calculators), and `cachesP` carries `.catch(()=>null)`.

### Ideas (grounded in what already exists)

- data/treasury-summary.json already holds the management wallet's native PLS and every token balance hourly (wallet1/wallet2 `tokenBalances`); the DAO panel's 9 `balanceOf` + 6 DexScreener price reads per PTGC visit (and the partial-failure problem in R-6) could be replaced by reading that file first and keeping the chain path as the refresh/fallback — same pattern the burn windows use (13495–13511).
- data/swap-volume.json carries per-pool hourly rows (`pools`) for the last 90 days; the Live Feed's one-off Top Pools scan (80 `eth_getLogs` + 80 `eth_getBlockByNumber` on first open) could seed from it and scan only since `generatedAt`.
- The portfolio's 7 single `eth_call`s per wallet are the same shape as `pfhRpc`'s batches — one `pfhRpc([...6 calls])` per wallet is a drop-in and would make a 20-wallet mount 20 posts instead of 140+.
- The pipeline's `build-value-generated.mjs` and index's `fetchDeliveredValue` already share one helper body (gotcha 4); sharing the PAIR LIST the same way (three constructor pools for Mint, written into value-generated.json `meta.pairs`) would stop the browser fallback from scanning pools the contract never touches (R-4).
- A tiny `fetchData(url, ms)` (fetch + AbortController + `r.ok` + JSON) used by all 30-odd raw.githubusercontent reads across the five pages would retire the hang class in R-1/R-2/R-3 in one place and make the c12/`?t=` policy a single line.

Everything below was measured on the clone (`/home/claude/audit/repo`). The live site is unreachable from this sandbox, so
network numbers come from the repo files (gzip -9), the pinned npm packages in `tools/harness/node_modules`, and headless
Chromium runs through a copy of the harness (`/home/claude/audit/work/P/run-p*.js` = `tools/harness/run.js` with absolute
paths, an optional CDP throttle and gzip on the local server; probes in the same folder). Compile timings are from this
container's CPU — read them as ratios.
